items-center justify-between w-full text-sm font-semibold text-muted-foreground cursor-pointer"><span class="flex items-center gap-2"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-list h-4 w-4"><path d="M3 12h.01"></path><path d="M3 height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-chevron-down h-4 w-4"><path d="m6 9 6 6 6-6"></path></svg></button></div></div><div><p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Large language model inference has traditionally operated on a simple premise: the user submits a complete prompt (request), the model processes it, and returns a response (either streaming or at once). This paradigm works well for text-based chatbots and batch processing workloads, but it falls short when dealing with realtime applications, such as streaming audio or video.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">vLLM has recently added support for <strong>streamable inputs</strong> to its engine as well as a <strong>Realtime WebSocket API</strong> building on top of it, exposing a new <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">/v1/realtime</code> endpoint in the server.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">In this post, we motivate the need for realtime inference and introduce the two new features in vLLM that unlock these capabilities: <strong>streaming input support</strong> and the <strong>Realtime WebSocket API</strong>.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><em>Note</em>: If you wish to know how to use the new streaming input or realtime API with vLLM, please refer to the following references:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/tree/main/tests/v1/streaming_input" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Streaming input</a></li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/serving/openai_compatible_server/?h=realtime+api#realtime-api" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Realtime WebSocket API</a></li>
</ul>
<h1 id="why-realtime-is-needed" class="text-3xl font-bold mt-10 mb-4 scroll-mt-24">Why Realtime is Needed</h1>
<h2 id="the-traditional-batch-paradigm-in-vllm" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">The Traditional Batch Paradigm in vLLM</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Traditional LLM inference assumes that complete prompts are available upfront. The user submits their full request <em>e.g.</em> via a <a href="https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">ChatCompletionRequest</code></a>, waits for the model to process it entirely, and then receives the complete response. While vLLM has long supported <em>output</em> streaming—emitting tokens as they are generated—the <em>input</em> side was always fixed: you had to provide the entire request before inference could begin.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">This approach is sufficient for most applications. Text-based chatbots, document summarization, and code generation all fit naturally into this model. But a growing class of applications cannot wait for complete input before processing begins.</p>
<h2 id="the-importance-of-streaming" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">The Importance of Streaming</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Consider a voice assistant to control a computer or phone. Instead of using a keyboard and mouse or touchpad, all actions are controlled by voice. Speech is recorded by a microphone and sent as a stream of audio to your LLM which acts as the voice assistant model.
The LLM needs to continuously process the audio stream and generate actions in realtime.
For such applications latency, or more precisely <a href="https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/time-to-first-token-ttft" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Time-To-First-Token (TTFT)</a>, matters—a user does not want to wait more than a second to open an application, type text into a search bar, etc. For the most natural, human-like voice assistant, it needs to be able to listen and speak at the same time, i.e., the LLM needs to be able to process the audio stream and generate actions simultaneously.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">A natural question is whether streaming behavior can be approximated using non-streaming LLMs by processing the input in chunks. In principle, audio can be buffered into segments, each segment processed independently, and the resulting outputs concatenated. In practice, this approach introduces several limitations. Achieving sub-second TTFT requires highly performant chunk detection, i.e., accurately determining when to segment the audio stream such that no relevant information is lost. Poor segmentation can lead to an increased TTFT or degrade model performance by fragmenting meaningful temporal context. Chunk-based processing also precludes true bidirectional interaction: each chunk must be fully processed before a response can be generated, preventing listening and speaking from occurring concurrently. This results in a turn-based interaction model rather than the continuous, overlapping communication characteristic of human conversation.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">This problem appears across many domains:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Voice assistants</strong> require sub-second response times to feel natural as described above</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Live transcription services</strong> need to display text as speech is recognized</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Robotics and embodied AI</strong> need to process continuous sensor streams (cameras, microphones, LIDAR) and generate control actions with minimal delay to interact safely with the physical world</li>
</ul>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">For these applications, the traditional batch paradigm introduces unacceptable delays. Infrastructure is needed that can process input incrementally and begin generating output before all input has arrived.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><em>Note</em>: Even for traditional applications, where the full input needs to be read in order to generate the first output token, the ability to stream input as it becomes available can still be beneficial.
By default, vLLM makes use of <a href="https://docs.vllm.ai/en/stable/cli/serve/?h=max+num+b#-enable-chunked-prefill-no-enable-chunked-prefill" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">chunked prefill</a> and therefore processes an input of <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:0" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" first prefill forward pass can be scheduled earlier.</p>
<h2 id="requirements-for-streaming" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Requirements for Streaming</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Not all models can support true streaming inference. Two key requirements must be met: the right attention pattern and training for incremental processing.</p>
<h3 id="attention-patterns" class="text-xl font-semibold mt-6 mb-2 scroll-mt-24">Attention Patterns</h3>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The attention mechanism determines whether a model can process input incrementally or must wait for the entire sequence.</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed">
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Causal attention (uni-directional mask) restricts each position <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.025ex" 433Q222 431 272 431H323Q330 424 330 420Q330 398 317 385H210L174 240Q135 80 135 68Q135 26 162 26Q197 26 230 60T283 144Q285 150 288 151T303 153H307Q322 153 322 145Q322 142 319 133Q314 117 301 95T267 48T216 6T155 -11Q125 -11 98 4T59 56Q57 64 57 83V101L92 241Q127 382 128 383Q128 385 77 385H26Z"></path></defs><g stroke="currentColor" fill="currentColor" stroke-width="0" transform="scale(1,-1)"><g data-mml-node="math"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D461" xlink:href="#MJX-9-TEX-I-1D461"></use></g></g></g></svg></mjx-container> arrives. This makes true streaming possible: each new token can be processed immediately, and earlier outputs never need to be revised.</p>
</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed">
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Bidirectional attention (full mask) allows every position to attend to both past and future tokens. As a result, the model&#x27;s output token at position <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.025ex" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="0.817ex" height="1.441ex" role="img" focusable="false" viewBox="0 -626 361 637" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink"><defs><path id="MJX-10-TEX-I-1D461" d="M26 385Q19 392 19 395Q19 399 22 411T27 425Q29 430 36 430T87 431H140L159 511Q162 522 166 540T173 566T179 586T187 603T197 615T211 624T229 626Q247 625 254 615T261 596Q261 589 252 549T232 470L222 433Q222 431 272 431H323Q330 424 330 420Q330 398 317 385H210L174 240Q135 80 135 68Q135 26 162 26Q197 26 230 60T283 144Q285 150 288 151T303 153H307Q322 153 322 145Q322 142 319 133Q314 117 301 95T267 48T216 6T155 -11Q125 -11 98 4T59 56Q57 64 57 83V101L92 241Q127 382 128 383Q128 385 77 385H26Z"></path></defs><g stroke="currentColor" fill="currentColor" stroke-width="0" transform="scale(1,-1)"><g data-mml-node="math"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D461" xlink:href="#MJX-10-TEX-I-1D461"></use></g></g></g></svg></mjx-container> is conditions on tokens that may not have arrived yet. Until the full input sequence is known, the model cannot compute a stable output for any position, because future tokens could change how earlier tokens are interpreted.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">For this reason, bidirectional attention inherently requires access to the complete input sequence before producing outputs, which makes it incompatible with streaming or online processing.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">For long-running or infinite streaming, standard causal attention is not enough. If each token attends to the entire past, computation and memory grow without bound, which is impractical. In practice, past context must be truncated.
A common architectural solution is sliding-window attention, where each token attends only to a fixed-size window of recent tokens, keeping compute and memory bounded while supporting streaming. Hence, causal attention with a sliding window is often the architecture of choice for modern streaming models.</p>
<h3 id="training-for-streaming-inputs" class="text-xl font-semibold mt-6 mb-2 scroll-mt-24">Training for Streaming inputs</h3>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">However, having a fully streamable architecture is not sufficient on its own: the model must also be trained to support <strong>true streaming input</strong>.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Let <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.566ex" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="19.923ex" height="2.262ex" role="img" <p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Instead, a streaming model must be able to predict <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.464ex" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" <p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">where <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.023ex" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="1.005ex" height="1.645ex" role="img" focusable="false" viewBox="0 -717 444 727" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink"><defs><path id="MJX-24-TEX-I-1D6FF" d="M195 609Q195 656 227 686T302 717Q319 716 351 709T407 697T433 690Q451 682 451 662Q451 644 438 628T403 612Q382 612 348 641T288 671T249 657T235 628Q235 584 334 463Q401 379 401 292Q401 169 340 80T205 -10H198Q127 -10 83 36T36 153Q36 286 151 382Q191 413 252 434Q252 435 245 449T230 481T214 521T201 566T195 609ZM112 130Q112 83 136 55T204 27Q233 27 256 51T291 111T309 178T316 232Q316 267 309 298T295 344T269 400L259 396Q215 381 183 342T137 256T118 179T112 130Z"></path></defs><g stroke="currentColor" fill="currentColor" stroke-width="0" transform="scale(1,-1)"><g data-mml-node="math"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D6FF" xlink:href="#MJX-24-TEX-I-1D6FF"></use></g></g></g></svg></mjx-container> is a lookahead parameter that should be as small as possible. In theory, <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.023ex" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="1.005ex" height="1.645ex" role="img" focusable="false" viewBox="0 -717 444 727" xmlns:xlink="http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink"><defs><path id="MJX-25-TEX-I-1D6FF" d="M195 609Q195 656 227 686T302 717Q319 716 351 709T407 697T433 690Q451 682 451 662Q451 644 438 628T403 612Q382 612 348 641T288 671T249 657T235 628Q235 584 334 463Q401 379 401 292Q401 169 340 80T205 -10H198Q127 -10 83 36T36 153Q36 286 151 382Q191 413 252 434Q252 435 245 449T230 481T214 521T201 566T195 609ZM112 130Q112 83 136 55T204 27Q233 27 256 51T291 111T309 178T316 232Q316 267 309 298T295 344T269 400L259 396Q215 381 183 342T137 256T118 179T112 130Z"></path></defs><g stroke="currentColor" fill="currentColor" stroke-width="0" transform="scale(1,-1)"><g data-mml-node="math"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D6FF" xlink:href="#MJX-25-TEX-I-1D6FF"></use></g></g></g></svg></mjx-container> could be set to zero; in practice, a small delay is usually necessary to achieve reasonable performance.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">As a result, training a streaming model requires:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>i)</strong> aligning input and output sequences such that <mjx-container class="MathJax" jax="SVG"><svg style="vertical-align:-0.186ex" <p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">An intuitive architecture, as pioneered by <a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.08753" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Delayed Streams Modeling</a> and picked up by <a class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline" href="TODO">Voxtral-Realtime</a>, sum-pools input embeddings (e.g., speech embeddings) and output embeddings (e.g., text embeddings) into a single sequence of embeddings. The model then predicts</p>

data-c="1D466" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D466"></use></g><g data-mml-node="mo" transform="translate(523,413) scale(0.707)"><use data-c="2032" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-V-2032"></use></g><g data-mml-node="mi" transform="translate(523,-247) scale(0.707)"><use data-c="1D458" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D458"></use></g></g><g data-mml-node="mo" transform="translate(1219.2,0)"><use data-c="3D" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-N-3D"></use></g><g data-mml-node="msub" transform="translate(2275,0)"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D466" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D466"></use></g><g data-c="2B" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-N-2B"></use></g><g data-mml-node="msub" transform="translate(4438.8,0)"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D465" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D465"></use></g><g data-mml-node="TeXAtom" transform="translate(605,-153.4) scale(0.707)" data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><g data-mml-node="mi"><use data-c="1D458" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D458"></use></g><g data-mml-node="mo" transform="translate(521,0)"><use data-c="2B" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-N-2B"></use></g><g data-mml-node="mi" transform="translate(1299,0)"><use data-c="1D6FF" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-I-1D6FF"></use></g></g></g><g data-mml-node="mo" transform="translate(6326.3,0)"><use data-c="2E" xlink:href="#MJX-32-TEX-N-2E"></use></g></g></g></svg></mjx-container>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">This distinction is important for deployment: one cannot simply take an arbitrary causal model and expect it to perform well in a streaming setting. To be fully streamable, the model must be explicitly trained with the above alignment and architectural constraints, ensuring that both conditions <strong>i)</strong> and <strong>ii)</strong> are satisfied.</p>
<h2 id="why-model-architecture-matters-for-serving" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Why Model Architecture Matters for Serving</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">vLLM can serve any model, but true streaming requires architecturally-causal models. Models like <a href="https://mistral.ai/news/voxtral" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Voxtral</a> are designed from the ground up for streaming, using causal attention mechanisms that support incremental processing.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Equally important, the serving infrastructure must support incremental input. Even with a streaming-capable model, if the server requires the complete prompt before beginning inference, you lose the latency benefits.
This is why vLLM now supports streaming input alongside its existing output streaming capabilities.</p>
<h3 id="further-reading-on-streaming-architecture" class="text-xl font-semibold mt-6 mb-2 scroll-mt-24">Further Reading on Streaming architecture</h3>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.02562" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Transformer Transducer</a> is a well-known and one of the most successful modeling approaches for training streamable speech recognition systems.</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08753" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Streaming Sequence-to-Sequence Learning with Delayed Streams Modeling</a> by the Kyutai folks is a great read on further diving into streaming architectures as explained above.</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.00033" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Streaming Simultaneous Speech Translation with Augmented Memory Transformer</a> on streaming speech translation. Translation is not as &quot;monotonic&quot; as speech, which makes the problem of performant streaming more difficult.</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://mistral.ai/news/voxtral" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Voxtral-Realtime</a> is a massively pretrained and open-sourced streaming model competitive with most offline speech recognition models.</li>
</ul>
<h1 id="streaming-input-support-in-vllm" class="text-3xl font-bold mt-10 mb-4 scroll-mt-24">Streaming Input Support in vLLM</h1>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">With <a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/28973" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">PR #28973</a>, vLLM now supports streaming input for inference. This enables the incremental processing described above, where input arrives over time and output is generated continuously.</p>
<h2 id="the-streaminginput-interface" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">The StreamingInput Interface</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The core abstraction is the <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">StreamingInput</code> dataclass:</p>
<figure data-rehype-pretty-code-figure=""><div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full font-mono" tabindex="0" data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default"><code data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default" style="display:grid"><span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> dataclasses </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> dataclass</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> vllm.inputs </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> PromptType</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> vllm.sampling_params </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> SamplingParams</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#D2A8FF;--shiki-light:#8250DF">@dataclass</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">class</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800"> StreamingInput</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">:</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    prompt: PromptType</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    sampling_params: SamplingParams </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">|</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE"> None</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E"> =</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE"> None</span></span></code></pre></div></figure>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Rather than passing a fixed prompt to <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">AsyncLLM.generate()</code>, you can now pass an <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">AsyncGenerator</code> that yields <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">StreamingInput</code> objects over time. Each <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">StreamingInput</code> contains the next input chunk to be appended to a cumulative prompt. Here is an example of how it can be used:</p>
<figure data-rehype-pretty-code-figure=""><div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full font-mono" tabindex="0" data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default"><code data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default" style="display:grid"><span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> asyncio</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> vllm.inputs.data </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> StreamingInput</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> vllm.v1.engine.async_llm </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> AsyncLLM</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">from</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> vllm.sampling_params </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> SamplingParams</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">async</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E"> def</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#D2A8FF;--shiki-light:#8250DF"> streaming_input_example</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">():</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    async_llm </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> AsyncLLM.from_engine_args(</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">...</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">)</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    </span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">    # Input queue can consume inputs in separate async task</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    input_queue </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> asyncio.Queue[list[</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">int</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">]]()</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    </span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">    async</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E"> def</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#D2A8FF;--shiki-light:#8250DF"> input_generator</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">():</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">        # Loop until empty list encountered =&gt; input finished</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">        while</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> new_tokens </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">:=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> input_queue.get():</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">            yield</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> StreamingInput(</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">prompt</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">new_tokens)</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    output_generator </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> async_llm.generate(</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">        prompt</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">input_generator(),</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">        sampling_params</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">SamplingParams(</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">temperature</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">0.0</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">, </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">max_tokens</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">1</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">),</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    )</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    </span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">    # Consume outputs</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">    async</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E"> for</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> output </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">in</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> output_generator:</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">        # ...</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">asyncio.run(streaming_input_example())</span></span></code></pre></div></figure>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">You can wait until the output corresponding to the last input has completed before sending the next input, but it&#x27;s not required (input chunks are queued internally). Termination of the input stream is indicated by exiting from the async input generator or closing it using the <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">aclose</code> function. The returned outputs generator won&#x27;t complete until all received inputs have been processed <em>and</em> the input generator has completed.</p>
<h2 id="how-it-works" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">How It Works</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Internally, vLLM handles streaming input by treating each chunk as a separate request with a cumulative prompt. As new chunks arrive, the engine:</p>
<ol class="my-4 ml-6 list-decimal space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed">Extends <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code> with <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens - 1</code> generated <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">output_tokens</code> as well as new incoming <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code>.</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed">Reuses all cached KV values</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed">Generates output tokens based on the current cumulative prompt and the indicated <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens</code></li>
<li class="leading-relaxed">Optionally discards output when new input arrives</li>
</ol>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">This design means that output tokens generated between input chunks may be revised as more context becomes available. The final output reflects the complete input.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Internally, vLLM implements streaming input through a <em>sticky session</em> mechanism. The first input chunk creates an <strong>anchor request</strong> that persists throughout the session. Subsequent chunks with the same internal request ID are queued and processed in order.</p>
<h3 id="the-anchor-request-pattern" class="text-xl font-semibold mt-6 mb-2 scroll-mt-24">The Anchor Request Pattern</h3>
<div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full " style="font-family:Menlo, Consolas, &quot;Liberation Mono&quot;, &quot;Courier New&quot;, monospace"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                           STREAMING SESSION                                 │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                             │
│   User&#x27;s AsyncGenerator              Scheduler                              │
│   ═══════════════════               ═════════                               │
│                                                                             │
│   ┌──────────────┐                                                          │
│   │   Chunk 1    │ ──────────────►  Add ANCHOR REQUEST                      │
│   │   [A, B, C]  │                  ┌────────────────────────────────┐      │
│   └──────────────┘                  │  Request (id=&quot;session_1&quot;)      │      │
│                                     │  ├── resumable: true           │      │
│                                     │  ├── max_tokens: 2             │      │
│                                     │  ├── streaming_queue: deque()  │      │
│                                     │  ├── status: RUNNING           │      │
│                                     │  └── prompt_token_ids: [A,B,C] │      │
│                                     └────────────────────────────────┘      │
│                                              │                              │
│                                              ▼                              │
│   ┌──────────────┐                  ┌────────────────┐                      │
│   │   Chunk 2    │                  │    ENGINE      │  Generating...       │
│   │   [D, E]     │ ─────┐           │  Processing    │  ──► Output: [X, Y]  │
│   └──────────────┘      │           └────────────────┘                      │
│                         │                                                   │
│                         ▼           Anchor busy? Queue it!                  │
│   ┌──────────────┐      │           ┌────────────────────────────────┐      │
│   │   Chunk 3    │      └────────►  │  streaming_queue:              │      │
│   │   [F, G]     │ ─────────────►   │  ┌───────┐ ┌───────┐           │      │
│   └──────────────┘                  │  │[D, E] │→│[F, G] │→ ...      │      │
│                                     │  └───────┘ └───────┘           │      │
│                                     └────────────────────────────────┘      │
│                                                                             │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                     WHEN ANCHOR FINISHES CURRENT CHUNK                      │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                                             │
│   Engine signals: chunk complete (stopped = True)                           │
│          │                                                                  │
│          ▼                                                                  │
│   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
│   │  _handle_stopped_request() pops first item from queue          │        │
│   │                                                                │        │
│   │  streaming_queue: [[D,E], [F,G]]  ──►  [[F,G]]                 │        │
│   │                      ▲                                         │        │
│   │                      │                                         │        │
│   │                    pop!                                        │        │
│   └──────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
│                          │                                                  │
│                          ▼                                                  │
│   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
│   │  _update_request_as_session(anchor, update=[D, E])             │        │
│   │                                                                │        │
│   │  BEFORE:                       AFTER:                          │        │
│   │  ┌───────────────────────┐     ┌───────────────────────────┐   │        │
│   │  │ prompt_token_ids:     │     │ prompt_token_ids:         │   │        │
│   │  │   [A, B, C]           │     │   [A, B, C, X, D, E]      │   │        │
│   │  │ _output_token_ids:    │ ──► │ _output_token_ids:        │   │        │
│   │  │   [X, Y]              │     │   []                      │   │        │
│   │  │ _all_token_ids:       │     │ _all_token_ids:           │   │        │
│   │  │   [A, B, C, X, Y]     │     │   [A, B, C, X, D, E]      │   │        │
│   │  │ num_computed_tokens: 4│     │ num_computed_tokens: 4    │   │        │
│   │  │ status: RUNNING       │     │ status: WAITING           │   │        │
│   │  └───────────────────────┘     └───────────────────────────┘   │        │
│   │                                                                │        │
│   │  Note: Y is DISCARDED (last sampled token, not yet computed)   │        │
│   │        Only X is kept (num_computed_tokens = 4, so [A,B,C,X])  │        │
│   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
│                          │                                                  │
│                          ▼                                                  │
│   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐        │
│   │  Anchor returns to waiting queue → scheduled again → ENGINE    │        │
│   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
│                                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
</code></pre></div>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>Why is the last token (Y) discarded for the next <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code>?</strong></p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">When receiving a resumable request, what we really care about is computing the KV cache for all <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code> as well as <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens - 1</code> generated tokens. Note that the user indicates with <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens</code> that <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens - 1</code> generated tokens of the first request are final and shall be re-used. The final token of the <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">output_token_ids</code> tensor is simply the result of the most recent forward pass, which does not yet have corresponding KV cache states. It is up to the user to decide what do to with it, but as it does not have any corresponding KV cache states, it will
be discarded for the <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code> of the updated anchor request.
Discarding it is essentially &quot;free&quot;: we&#x27;re not invalidating any cached state, and it would need to be recomputed anyway if we kept it.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">As is done in the <a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/blob/a2443de5fa4a0605607f6c3d9219022c7f6ac480/vllm/entrypoints/openai/realtime/connection.py#L209" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Realtime API</a>, in most application, <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens</code> shall be set to <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">1</code> so that each resumable request computes the KV cache states only for the <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">prompt_token_ids</code> and it is up to the user to decide how to make use of the generated single token in <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">output_token_ids</code>. As an example, for <a href="https://mistral.ai/news/voxtral" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">Voxtral Realtime</a>, the generated single token in <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">output_token_ids</code> will be combined with the incoming new audio chunk to form the next resumable request.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><em>Caveat:</em> Some models emit special stop tokens that the model requires to properly continue generation. In such cases, the scheduling logic needs to accommodate +1 token to recompute the stop token before processing the new input chunk.</p>
<h2 id="example-flow" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Example Flow</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">For illustrative purposes, multiple resumable requests with different <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">max_tokens</code> can be streamed as inputs.
In such a case, the generation logic would function as follows:</p>
<div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full " style="font-family:Menlo, Consolas, &quot;Liberation Mono&quot;, &quot;Courier New&quot;, monospace"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">Input chunks: ([A1, B1, C1], max_tokens=1), ([A2, B2], max_tokens=2), ([A3], max_tokens=2)

1. First chunk [A1, B1, C1] arrives
   -&gt; Model generates [D1]

2. Second chunk [A2, B2] arrives
   -&gt; Cumulative prompt: [A1, B1, C1, A2, B2] (D1 discarded)
   -&gt; Model generates [C2, D2, E2]

3. Third chunk [A3] arrives
   -&gt; Cumulative prompt: [A1, B1, C1, A2, B2, C2, D2, A3] (E2 discarded)
   -&gt; Model generates [C3, D3]

Output stream: D1, C2, D2, E2, C3, D3
</code></pre></div>
<h1 id="realtime-api-with-websockets" class="text-3xl font-bold mt-10 mb-4 scroll-mt-24">Realtime API with WebSockets</h1>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">While streaming input support provides the core capability, production applications need a convenient API for real-time communication. <a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/33187" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">PR #33187</a> introduces a WebSocket-based Realtime API inspired by <a href="https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/realtime" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">OpenAI&#x27;s Realtime API</a>.</p>
<h2 id="architecture" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Architecture</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The Realtime API provides a WebSocket endpoint that enables bidirectional streaming between clients and the vLLM server. Clients send audio data, and the server responds with transcribed text and model outputs.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The architecture consists of:</p>
<ol class="my-4 ml-6 list-decimal space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>WebSocket Client</strong>: Captures audio from microphone, sends chunks to server</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Realtime Handler</strong>: Receives WebSocket messages, converts to StreamingInput</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>AsyncLLM</strong>: Processes streaming input, generates output</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Response Stream</strong>: Sends generated tokens back through WebSocket</li>
</ol>
<h2 id="server-setup" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Server Setup</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Starting a vLLM server with Realtime API support:</p>
<figure data-rehype-pretty-code-figure=""><div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full font-mono" tabindex="0" data-language="bash" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default"><code data-language="bash" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default" style="display:grid"><span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">vllm</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069"> serve</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069"> mistralai/Voxtral-Mini-4B-Realtime-2602</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE"> --enforce-eager</span></span></code></pre></div></figure>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The server exposes a WebSocket endpoint at <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">ws://localhost:8000/v1/realtime</code>.</p>
<h2 id="client-example" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Client Example</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Here&#x27;s a basic client that streams an audio file and receives transcription:</p>
<figure data-rehype-pretty-code-figure=""><div class="relative group my-4 rounded-lg border bg-muted/50"><button class="absolute right-2 top-2 z-10 p-1.5 rounded-md bg-background/80 border border-border/50 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity cursor-pointer hover:bg-muted" aria-label="Copy code"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="24" height="24" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-copy h-3.5 w-3.5 text-muted-foreground"><rect width="14" height="14" x="8" y="8" rx="2" ry="2"></rect><path d="M4 16c-1.1 0-2-.9-2-2V4c0-1.1.9-2 2-2h10c1.1 0 2 .9 2 2"></path></svg></button><pre class="overflow-x-auto p-4 text-sm [&amp;&gt;code]:block [&amp;&gt;code]:w-fit [&amp;&gt;code]:min-w-full font-mono" tabindex="0" data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default"><code data-language="python" data-theme="github-dark-default github-light-default" style="display:grid"><span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> asyncio</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> base64</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> json</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> librosa</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">import</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> numpy </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">as</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> np</span></span>

<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">    &quot;&quot;&quot;Load audio file and convert to PCM16 @ 16kHz.&quot;&quot;&quot;</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">    audio, _ </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> librosa.load(audio_path, </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">sr</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">16000</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">, </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">mono</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">True</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">)</span></span>

<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">                &quot;audio&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">: base64.b64encode(chunk).decode()</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">            }))</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">        # Signal end of audio stream</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">        await</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> ws.send(json.dumps({</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;type&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">: </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;input_audio_buffer.commit&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">, </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;final&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">: </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">True</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">}))</span></span>
<span data-line=""> </span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#8B949E;--shiki-light:#6E7781">        # Receive transcription</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">        async</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E"> for</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> message </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">in</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> ws:</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">            data </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> json.loads(message)</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">            if</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> data[</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;type&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">] </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">==</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069"> &quot;transcription.delta&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">:</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">                print</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">(data[</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;delta&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">], </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">end</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">, </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FFA657;--shiki-light:#953800">flush</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">=</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#79C0FF;--shiki-light:#0550AE">True</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">)</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">            elif</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328"> data[</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;type&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">] </span><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">==</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069"> &quot;transcription.done&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">:</span></span>
<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#FF7B72;--shiki-light:#CF222E">                break</span></span>

<span data-line=""><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">asyncio.run(stream_audio_file(</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#A5D6FF;--shiki-light:#0A3069">&quot;audio.wav&quot;</span><span style="--shiki-dark:#E6EDF3;--shiki-light:#1F2328">))</span></span></code></pre></div></figure>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">This example demonstrates the core workflow for realtime audio streaming:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Load and convert audio</strong>: The audio file is loaded and converted to PCM16 format at 16kHz, which is the expected input format for the realtime API</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Establish WebSocket connection</strong>: Connect to the server&#x27;s <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">/v1/realtime</code> endpoint and send a <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">session.update</code> message to validate the model</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Stream audio in chunks</strong>: The audio is sent in 4KB chunks using <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">input_audio_buffer.append</code> messages, with <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">input_audio_buffer.commit</code> signals to mark the start and end of the stream</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Receive transcription incrementally</strong>: The server responds with <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">transcription.delta</code> messages containing partial transcriptions, which are printed in real-time until <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">transcription.done</code> is received</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><strong>Note on realtime behavior</strong>: While this example sends all audio before listening for transcriptions (for simplicity), the WebSocket protocol enables fully asynchronous communication—audio chunks can be sent and transcriptions received simultaneously. In a production realtime service, transcription would begin immediately as the first audio chunk arrives, with both sending and receiving happening concurrently for true low-latency speech recognition</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="message-types" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Message Types</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The Realtime API uses a message-based protocol. Key message types include:</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>Client to Server:</strong></p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">session.create</code>: Initialize a new session</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">input_audio_buffer.append</code>: Send audio data</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">input_audio_buffer.commit</code>: Signal end of audio input</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">response.create</code>: Request model response</li>
</ul>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>Server to Client:</strong></p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">session.created</code>: Session initialization confirmed</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">response.text.delta</code>: Incremental text output</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">response.audio.delta</code>: Incremental audio output (for TTS models)</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">response.done</code>: Response complete</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">error</code>: Error occurred</li>
</ul>
<h2 id="example-scripts" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Example Scripts</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">The vLLM repository includes ready-to-use example clients:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/examples/online_serving/openai_realtime_client/?h=realtime#openai-realtime-client" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">examples/online_serving/openai_realtime_client.py</a>: Basic WebSocket client</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed"><a href="https://docs.vllm.ai/en/latest/examples/online_serving/openai_realtime_microphone_client/#openai-realtime-microphone-client" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">examples/online_serving/openai_realtime_microphone_client.py</a>: Microphone integration</li>
</ul>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">These examples demonstrate how to capture audio from system microphone and stream it to vLLM in real time.</p>
<h2 id="performance-considerations" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Performance Considerations</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">An advantage of using the dedicated <code class="px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all">AsyncGenerator</code>-based session interface over just sending separate requests is that the KV cache for the session is preserved as-is. This is preferable to relying on vLLM&#x27;s automatic prefix caching because:</p>
<ul class="my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5">
<li class="leading-relaxed">It ensures that the corresponding cache blocks won&#x27;t be evicted while waiting for the next input chunk</li>
<li class="leading-relaxed">Prefix caching works at a block-level (typically 16 tokens), meaning that a small number of existing tokens would otherwise be re-computed for each new input</li>
</ul>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">However, this also means that additional care must be taken to avoid holding sessions open as they will be blocking the corresponding memory from being used by other requests, potentially harming overall capacity/throughput. Currently, vLLM will not preempt &quot;idle&quot; streaming input sessions - this behaviour will be improved in a future update.</p>
<h2 id="future-directions" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Future Directions</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">We are excited about the potential for streaming input support in vLLM. As more model providers open-source fully streamable model weights that are compatible with our input streaming design, we expect the ecosystem of realtime applications to grow significantly.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Since streaming input is still a novel capability in LLM serving, we anticipate adapting and extending our implementation to support a maximum number of different architectures and use cases. This includes exploring tighter integration with various audio and video encoders, optimizing the anchor request pattern for different latency requirements, and expanding support for multi-modal streaming scenarios.</p>
<h2 id="get-involved" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Get Involved</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">We encourage you to try out vLLM&#x27;s input streaming functionality and Realtime API. Your feedback is invaluable in helping us improve these features. Please share your experiences, report issues, or suggest enhancements on the <a href="https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="text-vllm-blue hover:underline">vLLM GitHub repository</a>.</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">We welcome feedback and contributions as we continue to develop vLLM&#x27;s real-time capabilities.</p>
<h2 id="acknowledgements" class="text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24">Acknowledgements</h2>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">Streaming input support and the Realtime API were made possible through collaborative efforts across multiple teams:</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>Meta:</strong> Joshua Deng, Jiatong Zhou, Zhuohan Li, Yu Luo, Jeremy Teboul</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>Mistral AI:</strong> Patrick von Platen, Andy Lo</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed"><strong>vLLM Team:</strong> Nick Hill, Roger Wang, Cyrus Leung, Nicolò Lucchesi, Woosuk Kwon</p>
<p class="my-4 leading-relaxed">We would also like to acknowledge other implementations of streaming input in vLLM: Tao He (Alibaba Qwen), Edward Wibowo (Brown University), Deepti Raghavan (Brown University), and Luis Gaspar Schroeder (UC Berkeley).</p><style>

leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"Large language model inference has traditionally operated on a simple premise: the user submits a complete prompt (request), the model processes it, and returns a response (either streaming or at once). This paradigm works well for text-based chatbots and batch processing workloads, but it falls short when dealing with realtime applications, such as streaming audio or video.\"}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[\"vLLM has recently added support for \",[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"streamable inputs\"}],\" to \",[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"Realtime WebSocket API\"}],\".\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 Needed\"}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"id\":\"the-traditional-batch-paradigm-in-vllm\",\"className\":\"text-2xl font-bold mt-8 mb-3 scroll-mt-24\",\"children\":\"The Traditional Batch Paradigm in The user submits their full request \",[\"$\",\"em\",null,{\"children\":\"e.g.\"}],\" via a \",[\"$\",\"a\",null,{\"href\":\"https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create\",\"target\":\"_blank\",\"rel\":\"noopener noreferrer\",\"className\":\"text-vllm-blue hover:underline\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"code\",null,{\"className\":\"px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-md bg-muted font-mono text-sm break-all\",\"children\":\"ChatCompletionRequest\"}]}],\", waits for the model to process it entirely, and then receives the complete response. While vLLM has long supported \",[\"$\",\"em\",null,{\"children\":\"output\"}],\" streaming—emitting tokens as they are generated—the \",[\"$\",\"em\",null,{\"children\":\"input\"}],\" side was always fixed: you had to provide the entire request before inference could begin.\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"This approach is sufficient for most applications. Text-based chatbots, document summarization, and code generation all fit naturally into this model. But a growing class of applications cannot wait for complete input before processing begins.\"}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"h2\",null,{\"id\":\"the-importance-of-streaming\",\"className\":\"text-2xl font-bold leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[\"Consider a voice assistant to control a computer or phone. Instead of using a keyboard and mouse or touchpad, all actions are controlled by voice. Speech is recorded by a microphone and sent as a stream of audio to your LLM which acts as the voice assistant model.\\nThe LLM needs to continuously process the audio stream and generate actions in realtime.\\nFor such applications latency, or more precisely \",[\"$\",\"a\",null,{\"href\":\"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/time-to-first-token-ttft\",\"target\":\"_blank\",\"rel\":\"noopener noreferrer\",\"className\":\"text-vllm-blue hover:underline\",\"children\":\"Time-To-First-Token (TTFT)\"}],\", matters—a user does not want to wait more than a second to open an application, type text into a search bar, etc. For the most natural, human-like voice assistant, it needs to be able to listen and speak at the same time, i.e., the LLM needs to be able to process the audio stream and generate actions simultaneously.\"]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"2e:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"A natural question is whether streaming behavior can be approximated using non-streaming LLMs by processing the input in chunks. In principle, audio can be buffered into segments, each segment processed independently, and the resulting outputs concatenated. In practice, this approach introduces several limitations. Achieving sub-second TTFT requires highly performant chunk detection, i.e., accurately determining when to segment the audio stream such that no relevant information is lost. Poor segmentation can lead to an increased TTFT or degrade model performance by fragmenting meaningful temporal context. Chunk-based processing also precludes true bidirectional interaction: each chunk must be fully processed before a response can be generated, preventing listening and speaking from occurring concurrently. This results in a turn-based interaction model rather than the continuous, overlapping communication characteristic of human conversation.\"}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"2f:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"This problem appears across many domains:\"}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"30:[\"$\",\"ul\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 ml-6 list-disc space-y-1.5\",\"children\":[\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",null,{\"className\":\"leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"Voice assistants\"}],\" require sub-second response times to feel natural as described above\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",null,{\"className\":\"leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"Live transcription services\"}],\" need to display text as speech is recognized\"]}],\"\\n\",[\"$\",\"li\",null,{\"className\":\"leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"Robotics and embodied AI\"}],\" need to process continuous sensor streams (cameras, microphones, LIDAR) and generate control actions with minimal delay to interact safely with the physical world\"]}],\"\\n\"]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"31:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"For these applications, the traditional batch paradigm introduces unacceptable delays. This makes true streaming possible: each new token can be processed immediately, and earlier outputs never need to be revised.\"]}],\"\\n\"]}],\"\\n\",\"$L97\",\"\\n\"]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"38:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"For this reason, bidirectional attention inherently requires access to the complete input sequence before producing outputs, which makes it incompatible with streaming or online processing.\"}]\n39:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":\"For long-running or infinite streaming, standard causal attention is not enough. If each token attends to the entire past, computation and memory grow without bound, which is impractical. In practice, past context must be truncated.\\nA common architectural solution is sliding-window attention, where each token attends only to a fixed-size window of recent tokens, keeping compute and memory bounded while supporting streaming. Hence, causal attention with a sliding window is often the architecture of choice for modern streaming models.\"}]\n3a:[\"$\",\"h3\",null,{\"id\":\"training-for-streaming-inputs\",\"className\":\"text-xl font-semibold mt-6 mb-2 scroll-mt-24\",\"children\":\"Training for Streaming inputs\"}]\n3b:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4 leading-relaxed\",\"children\":[\"However, having a fully streamable architecture is not sufficient on its own: the model must also be trained to support \",[\"$\",\"strong\",null,{\"children\":\"true streaming input\"}],\".\"]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"3c:[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"className\":\"my-4
